[[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, a lot has been written, a lot has been debated, a lot is unfortunately being seen through the media of this terrible war in Putin on European soil: a throng of death, destruction, death, and injury to millions of refugees. I was very drawn to the attention of the United Nations a few days ago, a very short time ago. He said that in a war like that of Syria, which lasted two years, there were a million refugees. In Ukraine, in just over 30 days, there are more than four million refugees and six million internally displaced persons. That is the magnitude of the disaster that Putin has caused: a country devastated by indiscriminate bombings against the civilian population. All this does not fail to reveal the cruel result of a behavior that I would judge as anti-political - no doubt it is - of an expansionist and imperialist anxiety on the part of Putin, of a way of making totalitarian and inhuman politics. All that, ladies and gentlemen, all that, but I think something else. Let us not be mistaken, let us not be deceived and let us look at the reality of the challenge that Putin is taking to Europe. Putin's attack on Ukraine is Putin's attack on Europe, it is the desperate attempt to end or at least to weaken as much as possible a political project based on democracy, respect for human rights, safeguarding national law and international law as well as a society based on social justice. If democracy is consolidated in Ukraine, Putin will think, why can't tomorrow take place in Russia? Putin, therefore, ladies and gentlemen, attacks Europe because Putin fears Europe. All this leads me to share with all of you, and also with the whole of Spanish citizenship, two reflections. The first is that Putin's war takes place on a scene of pain and death 3000 kilometers away from Spain. There may legitimately be citizens in our country who think that, in geographical terms, that is far from it. It is a long way off, but its impact is, first of all, on our consciences and also on the European and Spanish economy, as we are seeing in the face of rising prices. This war has costs, without a doubt, ladies and gentlemen; we are already paying them in the form of a higher cost of living-or of living, in the case of the Ukrainians - but doing nothing, looking elsewhere, allowing Putin to crush Ukraine and meet its vile objectives would be the end of Europe. The cost we pay today is expensive - it is, ladies and gentlemen - but it would be even greater if we didn't stop Putin's feet. It is Europe that is at stake, its present and its future, our present and the future of our children. The second reflection is as follows. Ladies and gentlemen, as you know, Spain joined the European Union in the 1980s. Then I was about fifteen years old. [[Several ladies and gentlemen: Oooh!- Rumors-Mr Cerdán León claps his cheek with his hand, addressing the seats of the People's Parliamentary Group in Congress]] Thank you; thank you because, after two years of pandemic and now a war, where the foundations of our democracy are being attacked, we have reason to be proud of being European, and not for being born at a specific geographical point in Europe, or by any feature of our physiognomy, or by any other accident of nature. So, ladies and gentlemen, the conclusion of these two reflections is unity; unity. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, unity in Spain, unity in Europe and also unity in our transatlantic link, because this year, as you all know, is the 40th anniversary of our accession to the Atlantic Alliance. Next June, a NATO summit will be held that is very important for NATO's history and also for our country, which will define the new NATO strategy until 2030. You can see that even this war in Putin, there were judicious analyses of think tanks predicting the end of NATO; even non-minor political leaders of the European Union came to talk about the brain death of NATO. Well, I believe that the war in Putin has reminded us of the need for this alliance between States to defend and safeguard peace on our borders. Once again - let us not forget, ladies and gentlemen - the lesson that together we are stronger, that united we are more sure, that united we guarantee peace on our borders. Ladies and gentlemen, let's not fall into that trap. Putin knows that Russia was never threatened by an invasion or a war conflict by NATO, nor by Ukraine; he is fully aware of this. What he has done has been to use Ukraine's legitimate demand - which is a free, sovereign people - to enter NATO and also to belong to the European Union to attack and materialise its imperialist aspirations. And in addition, with it, cut off all will on the part of the Ukrainian people to consolidate a democracy outside the Russian orbit. He already did, by the way, years ago with the Maidan revolution. In case there is any doubt on this issue, I would like to recall the statements that all NATO members have made over the course of this long month since the beginning of the invasion. What has been done by all, including by the Secretary-General of NATO, has been to reject the sending of troops to Ukrainian soil, to reject the closure of Ukraine's airspace, as called for by President Zelensky, but which would obviously lead us all to a larger-scale conflict and to warn that NATO alone - I repeat, alone - would respond exclusively to an attack on one of its member countries. Ladies and gentlemen, no one wants this war. No one wants her except one person. The Ukrainians do not want it, the Europeans do not want it, the allies of the Atlantic Alliance do not want it, and I am convinced that if they asked the Russian people precisely, they would not want this war either. Now, both Europe and the Atlantic Alliance will support a people who defend themselves from a brutal invasion that seeks only the occupation and destruction of Ukraine, and we do it at European level, we do it at transatlantic level and we do it at bilateral level. Viewed in perspective, ladies and gentlemen, after listening to many of you in your speeches, and also much of the debate that has taken place over the last few weeks in the media, it is superfluous, if I may say so, any distinction between defensive and offensive weapons, because, in the face of any aggression, all weapons are defensive. Spain supplies the resistance and the Ukrainian military with weapons for their self-defence because they are being attacked. In addition to military support, it is essential, as you know, to respond to an unprecedented humanitarian crisis, at least since World War II. As I said before, in just four weeks - I insist, in just four weeks - almost four million Ukrainian refugees have left their country and the United Nations estimates that there are about six and a half million internally displaced human beings waiting to see what the future of the war conflict is in their country. Precisely at the NATO summit held in Brussels on 24 March, the urgent task of acting in solidarity and articulating joint plans to respond to this humanitarian crisis is what has become apparent. In this regard, I would like to say that Spain has approved, as you know, a humanitarian aid package of EUR 31 million for Ukraine and also for neighbouring countries that have been receiving this massive flow of refugees, the largest, I insist, since the Second World War. I also have a strong will for our country to provide an exemplary response in this regard, as we also did to the evacuation of refugees and United Nations and international organizations from Afghanistan. This is a moral imperative; the moment demands our solidarity and Spain will again set an example. [[Applauds]] In the face of the invasion of Putin, the Atlantic Alliance has always had clear support for the independence, sovereignty and territorial integrity of Ukraine, in accordance with internationally recognized borders, and we have also supported all efforts to strengthen neighbouring nations that are suffering the effects of the interference and threat of Putin, such as Moldova, Georgia and also the Western Balkans, in particular Bosnia and Herzegovina. In short, countries that are receiving a great many refugees in proportion to their population. That support must also go hand in hand with the unequivocal condemnation of Putin and his war, as it has, by the way, happened in a Newborn vote in the United Nations General Assembly and also with the request of more than forty countries to the International Criminal Court to investigate alleged war crimes, crimes against humanity and genocide by the troupers in Ukraine. Spain is, of course, among the applicant countries. Ladies and gentlemen, there have been many things in this area that Putin has also reminded us, especially the Europeans, how important it is to defend peace. If the European border is also the Spanish border and, therefore, the border of Poland and the Baltic countries is also the Spanish border, we must all be responsible, united and reinforce those borders when they are in danger, as is the case here. Because deterrence is fundamental. For this reason, NATO has activated its plans, strengthened the eastern flank and launched numerous units under NATO direct command. Within this framework, it has been decided to establish four new tactical groups in Bulgaria, Romania, Hungary and Slovakia. All the measures being taken are preventive, proportionate to the challenge we face, prudent, to ensure the security of the allies with a three hundred and sixty-degree approach. We believe that the reinforcement carried out so far on the eastern flank is the right one and we support the studies to define the future position. We are at a moment, ladies and gentlemen, fundamental, transcendental in our history. I do not want to be more dramatic than what we are seeing in the media, but we are facing a much more dangerous strategic reality than we have had so far, and to that end we must also adapt NATO's strategy over the coming years. And that is precisely what we are going to do, all united, at the Madrid Summit, where the new strategic concept for NATO will be collected until 2030. There is unanimity in many things, ladies and gentlemen, but above all in perceiving that the summit in Spain will be transcendental to the history of the Alliance, will mark the future of the organisation and will therefore define the security and protection of our countries. The Madrid Summit will provide an opportunity to significantly strengthen deterrence in the medium and long term, developing and increasing the full range of forces and capabilities available to ensure credible deterrence and defence. These developments must be flexible enough to adapt to the evolution of the strategic environment in the coming years. We will have to build a collective defence based on a thorough prior analysis that guarantees its realism and also its financial sustainability, taking into account all the threats, no doubt also the terrorist. Spain agrees, ladies and gentlemen, to strengthen deterrence on the eastern flank, I have heard it on other occasions, but it also believes that we must incorporate the challenges and challenges from the south, and more specifically from a region which is very important for stability, especially in the area of combating terrorism, migration, such as the Sahel, whose fragility and instability are a source of logical concern for European security. Steps are also being taken to increase the resilience of our societies, for example by strengthening our capacity to respond to misinformation and improve our cybersecurity capabilities - to which I will then refer - through the encouragement of mutual support in the event of attacks and the possibility of punishing those responsible. Other measures are aimed at improving preparedness to counter chemical threats, which are currently being discussed by the allies in relation to the threat that Russia is clearly posing; to counter chemical, biological, radiological and, of course, nuclear threats. Ladies and gentlemen, all this brings me to a reflection, and it is that the defence of peace requires adequate resources. This war has mobilized all the allies and there are many countries - I would say not the majority, but the whole of the European countries - that have already announced significant efforts to improve their defence, and now it is peremptory to accelerate those efforts. Our commitment as a Member State of the European Union and also of NATO is that of a State that is reliable, that with responsibility and solidarity in ensuring the security of all our borders, not only the next, but also the distant, responds to the challenges it faces and makes it impossible, ladies and gentlemen, something I have said on previous occasions and is to increase our defence budget. It is our duty to defend that 2% of our gross domestic product over the next few years. I am not talking about a sudden increase, because the ministry itself would not have the capacity to absorb all this enormous resource of financial capabilities, but rather progressive. I am simply stating evidence, ladies and gentlemen, and we must draw conclusions from this war. Our security is threatened and that is why it has to be strengthened, and all this has a cost, which, of course, we will accompany with our national priorities, which, as you know, have a strong social accent. However, we will continue to invest in our capabilities above the commitment made at the NATO Wales Summit of 20% of the total budget, which will allow us to push something very important, which is the defence industry. We will also maintain our high commitment to the operations and missions of the Alliance. There is no militaristic velocity, ladies and gentlemen, for this purpose. Conversely, we only want to defend our democracy and our peace against the enemies of European peace and security. In short, Putin's war represents a fundamental challenge to our principles and values that have brought that, security and prosperity to the continent for many decades. However, Putin's decision to attack Ukraine is a strategic mistake with serious consequences not only for Ukraine or European countries, but for the entire international community. Here are the recent OECD data on the impact this Putin war is going to have on global economic growth. A few days ago, an African President told me precisely: do not just talk about European security, which Putin is questioning; we are talking about global security. Indeed, we are talking about global security, but it also has serious consequences for Russia and its own people. I hope that the diplomatic efforts that we are seeing, and which today precisely open a window to hope, will come to a good place. I insist, no one wants war except one person, and from the looks of it, neither is the war going as well as it initially would have expected. But let us not be naive: there was no lack of diplomacy, but aggression remained. And as long as that diplomatic route doesn't go ahead, it's essential to be united in deterrence. In any case, ladies and gentlemen, if anyone has opened their eyes Putin is Europeans and Europeans. Ladies and gentlemen, until last 24 February, which was the date of the beginning of the invasion, I believe that the European Union naively believed - now we see it - that we could be a global power without a high capacity for deterrence in defence. The European Union also believed that gas, oil and coal could be safely supplied from a regime like Putin. It is paradoxical, ladies and gentlemen, that energy and peace, the two elements that defined the creation of what we now call the European Union, are the two elements that Putin has put at risk with this war; peace and energy. In any case, ladies and gentlemen, Europe has understood the message, has taken note and has begun to act. We've done it together again, all together. On energy, we are talking about energy security on a double side, we are not only talking about security of supply, but we are also talking about affordable prices, because there can be no security without supply and also without affordable prices. With regard to security of supply and diversification, I would like to point out that the Union, at its meeting of Heads of State and Government in Versailles on 10 and 11 March, we took the decision to end as soon as possible with Russia's energy dependence, which makes us no doubt much more vulnerable to the energy blackmail of Putin, which provides international currencies precisely to finance the war in Europe, and that decision will be welcomed in the conclusions of the formal European Council last week in Brussels. The European Union has decided to drastically - drastically - steadily and as quickly as possible reduce Russia's imports of gas, oil and coal, I insist, immediately. The Union is committed to speeding up the green energy transition, and Spain will undoubtedly be at the forefront of this determined commitment to energy autonomy, the response to climate change and, ultimately, the extension and presence of green energies in our energy matrix. [[Applauds]] In any case, ladies and gentlemen, for Spain this reduction in energy dependence on Russia is not as serious as for other countries. We are talking that just over 8% of the gas we consume in our country comes from Russia. We are talking about percentages that are infinitely lower than those that can have economies as important as Germany, which depends on 40% of Russian gas supply. In any case, this does not mean that we are more or less exposed to this war and its economic and social consequences. We are seeing it, I insist, again in the rise of energy prices and also of the cost of living. We all feel affected in one way or another, directly or indirectly, by the effects of war. Ladies and gentlemen, Spain has one in three regasifiers in Europe. It wants to make them available to our European partners - we have said this in many debates - once we are able to complete those interconnections that we have always talked about with France and must also serve to transport the energy of the future for which the Spanish Government is going to bet, which is the green hydrogen, that is, the hydrogen that comes from renewable energy sources. As you know, Spain has taken a leading position in the matter of energy prices for months. Proposals such as the centralized purchase of gas or acting on what is called in Spanish the fallen profits from the sky of the large electricity companies, which was labeled by some of your ladies and gentlemen as little less than chavista or interventionist, are today assumed by the communiqués of the European Commission, and are Spanish proposals. [[Applauds]] We agree on energy measures at European level which are extraordinarily complex, ladies and gentlemen, and I am convinced that we are going to talk about this precisely during the debate, but to talk about a single, common solution for the whole of the European Union, which is what Spain wants, is extraordinarily complex and difficult, and the simple reason is that the Union is composed of twenty-seven Member States. We share values, a common project, but there are 27 Member States with different characteristics and, in this case, with very different energy mixes and interconnection levels. It is not the same as Belgium, as the Netherlands, as Germany, in interconnections as the Iberian peninsula, which has an interconnection of 2%, when the target in the year 2020 of the European Union was 10% and in 2030 of 15%. We're at 2%. It can therefore be said that Spain and Portugal, the Iberian peninsula, are, although integrated into the European energy market, an energy island. And this, ladies and gentlemen, is what the Spanish Government has defended, in the absence of finding a common European position; given the complexity of finding precisely that common European solution, at least allowing Spain and Portugal, i.e. the Iberian peninsula, to act with complementary levers to which they are now established within the European energy framework. In any case, ladies and gentlemen, the European Union needs time to assimilate the complexity of these matters, to choose the right path, and this is precisely what is happening now. With great perseverance, ladies and gentlemen, the Spanish Government has endeavoured to convince our European partners, and it was precisely at the last European Council that we managed to get that consensus started. In the last six months - I have been told on many occasions - the Commission has issued three communications on this subject, increasingly approaching the proposals of Spain. The last, on 23 March, dedicated already to security of supply and also to affordable prices, where the Spanish proposals were included among the viable options to control the rise of prices. During the last few weeks I have proposed to explain the Spanish proposal to our European partners, country by country. The approach that I have defended, which the Spanish Government has defended, focused on three steps to meet the challenge of high energy prices, and they will allow me to explain those three steps. Firstly, urgent measures to apply immediately, as I said earlier, to non-interconnected energy islands such as the Iberian peninsula. Secondly, to put in place measures to control the price of gas on international markets, such as joint purchases, and also to discourage those third countries from the price at which gas is put and, therefore, not only to buy gas from a European perspective, but also to transfer the maximum price at which Europeans are willing to buy gas from those third countries. Thirdly, the need to move forward in the medium term towards a structural reform of the energy market. This energy market made sense thirty or forty years ago, in the 1990s, when the cost of renewable energy was even higher than that of gas, but today, on the other hand, it is clear that renewable energy is financially far more competitive than gas; therefore, we must ensure that the evolution of the gas price is not linked to the evolution of the electricity price. Therefore, three steps: firstly, measures to be taken immediately for the particular case of energy islands not interconnected to the energy market, such as the Iberian peninsula; secondly, measures to control the price of gas by centralised purchases and also with those reference prices that we place on the gas producing countries; and, thirdly, a structural reform of our energy market to somehow adapt it more to the energy reality of Europe or at least to where we want to go, which is precisely towards this energy transition and towards a preponderance of renewable energy, energy efficiency and energy saving in our continent. In that first step that took place on Monday, when we got Spain and Portugal, that is, the Iberian peninsula, to see their status as an energy island recognized, I want to thank the work not only of the European Commission but also of the Portuguese Government. In the coming weeks, exceptional temporary measures will be put in place to reduce electricity prices for consumers and businesses, and I am convinced that they will have a very rapid impact not only on developments in electricity prices but also on the cost of living in our country. We have been working for weeks now, but this week we are going to send our package of measures to the European Commission, which will urgently confirm - I am convinced - that these measures are in line with European law, taking into account these special conditions, I insist, of Spain and Portugal, such as the low level of electricity interconnection with Europe. These measures, ladies and gentlemen - it is very important that they are at least recorded - do not involve subsidies to gas, do not distort incentives to clean energy - that energy transition with which the Spanish Government is firmly committed - nor electricity flows between countries. In short, our mechanism does not distort the European electricity market, it will not pose any risk to the achievement of the European Union's energy transition and supply security objectives, but it will allow us to lower energy prices. In addition to this important step, I would say that, historically, this Council has given political impetus to all initiatives aimed at controlling the price of gas, which will now be compared with all stakeholders in the sector, with businesses, with partnerships, with NGOs and, logically, will also be a joint exercise that the European Commission is going to do with the European Council. That is to say, ladies and gentlemen, we will continue to make parallel progress in that second step, to which I referred earlier, and which many States - also the Government of Spain - have defended, such as Italy, Greece, Belgium, Slovenia, Slovakia, Romania and Bulgaria. In short, ladies and gentlemen, I believe that this European Council has been a turning point on the issue of energy, and we must congratulate ourselves on that. Now we can definitely support citizens and businesses in the face of rising prices and continue to work to consolidate energy security throughout the European Union. I would like to refer to the other element under discussion, ladies and gentlemen, which is once again European defence, which was the subject of debate at the European Council in Brussels on 24 and 25 March. Ladies and gentlemen, there is not a broad consensus, but a unanimous consensus, of the 100 % of the Member States of the European Union, that we must raise the budget in defence of the Member States of the Union to 2% in the coming years. The road map on which to deploy this new defence strategy is known as the so-called strategic compass. This is a key document, which has already been adopted, on the common security and defence policy for the next ten years; a compass that will serve to deepen our security and defence policy, but this must not be done in isolation - at least this is what the Spanish Government has defended - but we have to work with our non-EU partners, and that is why it is very important that the complementarity in NATO's defence strategy, as well as that of the European Union, is decisive. Spain will underline - it has always done so - that complementarity between the two organizations at the Madrid Summit next June. An important element that was also discussed at this last European Council - and it is probably the crisis that the world is going to suffer over the coming weeks and months - is that of food security. Spain had already stressed at the informal European Council in Versailles the importance of this issue not only for the European Union, but also for the world because of the weight that both Russia and Ukraine, you know, have on the global markets, for example, for cereals. The European Commission has already put forward proposals in support of farmers and consumers on our continent, on which we agree to continue working with the ultimate objective of strengthening our strategic autonomy, including in the food sector. In short, ladies and gentlemen, European unity in the response to the war in Putin, European unity condemning the invasion, sanctioning its regime, solidarity with Ukraine, welcoming refugees and deploying humanitarian aid, and unity, as well, ladies and gentlemen, in the response to the economic and social consequences of the war, as we also did during the pandemic, because I firmly believe, ladies and gentlemen, that Europe will also be strengthened from this clash. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, Madam President, war, pandemics, this is the context. It is true that these are difficult times, these are complex times, ladies and gentlemen, but these are the coordinates on which we live and on which we have been governed: an exceptional health emergency, with a great economic impact, and an unjustified war with terrible human and social consequences. With a pandemic in its phase of overcoming, in full economic recovery, thanks also to European funds, this war has arrived, which only wants one person, but which tests the whole of Europe, all the Member States and, therefore, our society as well. The Government, ladies and gentlemen, knows these difficulties, knows the problems of the affected sectors, the anxiety and uncertainty about a war that has only one culprit, which is Putin. We know these problems, we take care of them, we know them sector by sector and we also know them as a whole. We would like them not to exist, we would like them to disappear, never to have happened, but unfortunately the war and its consequences are not in the hands of this government or any European government. No one, ladies and gentlemen, is protected from this war. We cannot promise that we will not be aware of the effects of war on our economy and our lives, and we are seeing it in the evolution of prices, we are seeing it in the increase of the cost of living, but what we can promise from the Government of Spain is that we are going to dislive ourselves by maximising the effects of war on our population. [[Applauds]] We can also promise from the Spanish Government that we will distribute the sacrifices in a balanced manner so that no sector, and above all no family, especially the most vulnerable, feel neglected or abandoned to their fate. That is why yesterday's Council of Ministers approved the war response shock plan, with powerful measures that will serve to protect the most affected sectors and citizens, to distribute more fairly the effects of the war and also to preserve to the maximum the path of economic growth and job creation that we had already begun months ago. This plan contains measures in five areas of action. First, measures to help families, workers, displaced persons, and also humanitarian aid. Secondly, measures to support the economic and business fabric affected by this war. Thirdly, transport measures. Fourthly, cybersecurity measures. And fifthly, energy measures. It is a plan with an investment of 6 billion euros until next 30 June in direct aid, in tax cuts, in addition to 10 billion euros, that will be transferred through ICO credits, with which we intend to cushion the impact of the crisis on the most vulnerable families, businesses and groups. If I may, entering the first bloc on families, workers and displaced persons and humanitarian aid, I would like to highlight the following measures. Firstly, as you know, a minimum bonus of 20 cents per litre of fuel until 30 June. The government is going to take a 15 cent cut, the oil companies, a minimum of 5 cents, and some of them have already announced even higher cuts, which I would like to thank from this rostrum. Secondly, and exceptionally, in order to avoid high increases that would harm tenant families, rent revisions over the next three months will have a limit of 2 %. Thirdly, we have increased the minimum living income by 15% over three months. It reminded me yesterday precisely the minister that the maximum recipients of this minimum living income are above all boys and girls. You know that we have more than two million children suffering from child poverty, and it is therefore fully justified that we increase minimum living income by 15%. [[Applauds]] Fourthly, more than 600,000 families will be able to benefit from the electric social bonus, rising from 1 200 000 to 1 900 000 households, i.e. almost 2 000 000 households in Spain protected against rising prices and their translation, in fact, to the price of electricity in our country. Fifthly, it is clear that companies receiving aid under the shock plan will not be able to fire on objective grounds; on the contrary, we have, as you know, encouraged, through this labour reform approved by the Courts, measures of internal flexibility alternative to dismissal, such as ERTE. Finally, as I announced in my last appearance, we have extended, with an extraordinary fiscal effort, until 30 June, the tax rebates linked to the bill of light, which we approved many months ago, even before Putin's invasion. I am referring to VAT, which will continue to be 10% for small consumers, the excise duty on electricity, 0.5%, or the suspension of the excise duty on electricity generation. When we talk about tax cuts, ladies and gentlemen, this government lowers taxes, but lowers taxes where it has to lower them, which is at the price of light, with 60% tax cuts at the price of light. [[Applauds]] If we talk about vulnerability, it is impossible not to refer to one of the most dramatic realities of the war that we see through the media, I insist, the millions of displaced persons and refugees who also arrive in our country. Spain, as you know, has activated the temporary protection directive. We will allocate the resources necessary for the reception of refugees from Ukraine. We have simplified the procedures of the reception system,we have enabled three reception centres, one in Madrid, the other in Barcelona and the other in Alicante. Soon we will open a room in the city of Malaga. There is no doubt that the national reception system, originally designed for a limited number of people, not for this large number of refugees, has experienced exponential growth in applications in recent times, and that is why what we have done has been to go beyond the plan and to adopt the regulations governing that system through concerted action between entities and Administration, in order to make the reception process much more stable and much more predictable. As you know, in the specific case of Ukrainian refugees, the Government has extended the assumptions laid down by the European directive on the granting of residence permits and also work permits; today there are already more than 27 000 refugees who have applied for temporary protection in Spain and there are more than 13,000 places occupied in the national reception system, which will be continuously expanded if the flow of refugees continues to increase, as we are seeing in recent days. Now the plan also adds the flexibility of the requirements in the nationality per residence files requested by Ukrainian citizens, along with other actions, such as the free rail transport for these refugees. And the plan, ladies and gentlemen, to conclude, includes other measures such as facilitating type-approval, the validation of studies abroad, promoting the integration of victims of trafficking and specific care for victims of violence against women in the context of humanitarian crises. The second axis of the response plan has to do with the economic and business fabric. As I said to you, in the case of the pandemic, the first concern is to support the liquidity of our companies in the face of the threat, which is, hopefully, short-term. In this case we have the extraordinary precedent of the ICOs' endorsements, which I believe have been an outstanding experience throughout the pandemic, which were essential to save hundreds of thousands of businesses, especially small and medium-sized enterprises, self-employed workers, in the first moments of confinement. This is why the response plan envisages the implementation of a new ICO credit line, worth EUR 10 billion, covering liquidity needs caused by increased energy costs and also fuel costs. It also extends the maturity of previous ICO-backed loans in order to continue to guarantee precisely the viability of the companies that we welcome at this exceptional time. In relation to industry, which is the largest energy consumer, the volume of resources has been increased to compensate for tolls by 80 % to reach the maximum of CO2 compensation. Direct aid to the sector has also been strengthened, which will mean a total package, ladies and gentlemen, of more than EUR 500 million for the electro-intensive industry. We have also approved an important aid package of EUR 362 million - I repeat, EUR 362 million - for the agriculture and livestock sector, and another EUR 68 million for the fisheries and aquaculture sector. [[Applauds]] Finally, the plan includes many other measures that may emerge in the debate, such as, for example, support for the internationalisation of Spanish companies in a global environment of extraordinary uncertainty, as is evident, as well as a number of measures and actions in sectors at best specific, such as the cultural sector. Thirdly, the war response plan sets out a series of transport measures that you know because they have been discussed and also in agreement with the transport sector. I am fully aware, ladies and gentlemen, that the Government is fully aware of the difficulties it is going through in the freight transport sector and also in the passenger sector; difficulties which have already been dragging away since the summer of 2021 and which are fully linked to the rise in fuel prices. These difficulties also hamper any possibility of sustainable recovery of businesses, micro-symes and self-employed people who have already had a very bad time during the pandemic, who worked very hard precisely to meet the needs of our homes while we were all confined; or, as is the case with the transport of travellers, who were able to resist when mobility was stopped as a result of the measures we had to take to stop the spread of the virus. The Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda has met with representatives of the freight department of the National Road Transport Committee to address the current situation in the sector. Different measures have been taken that have been agreed with the main partnerships in the sector. And in the field of road transport, the measures will amount to nothing more and nothing less than an injection of more than EUR 1 billion, and they are measures linked to the following paragraphs. For example, first of all, carriers will benefit most from the bonus of at least 20 cents per litre or kilo of fuel. Secondly, a €450 million fund will be set up for freight and passenger transport companies. These aids are to be established on the basis of the type of vehicle. For example, EUR 1250 per truck, EUR 900 per bus, EUR 500 per van and EUR 300 per light vehicle, i.e. taxis, ambulances or VTC. And thirdly, the hydrocarbon tax will be refunded monthly to increase the liquidity of professionals at this time so complicated that they are going through. [[Applauds]] Finally, as we did with the food chain law, we will pass a fair price law for carriers. Fourthly, ladies and gentlemen, the national plan for responding to war includes cybersecurity measures that are very relevant. Ladies and gentlemen, I believe that we are all very aware that the war in Ukraine is an increase in the risk of cyberattacks that can put the operation of critical infrastructures, public administrations, private companies or university centres at risk and at risk. For this reason, the response plan includes urgent actions in the area of cybersecurity focused on three axes. The first is the adoption of the National Cybersecurity Plan, which is particularly ambitious, both in its budget allocation, with more than EUR 1 billion, and in the variety and scope of the measures. We are looking at more than 150 essential actions to ensure cybersecurity in our country. Secondly, the establishment of a cybersecurity operations centre of the General Administration of the State and its public bodies. And third, strengthening the security of the new 5G electronic communications networks, which are crucial to driving the digital revolution we are experiencing. To this end, the 5G Cybersecurity Bill, which will set out the requirements for deployment and operation of the 5G Networks, will be dealt with as a matter of urgency, logically in line with European legislation. And fifth and last, the national plan contains energy-related measures. The first of these, the most important of all because of its importance, is that adopted at the European Council, which, as I said earlier, recognises the uniqueness of the Iberian peninsula as an energy island and, therefore, the possibility of taking extraordinary and temporary measures to reduce electricity prices in Spain and Portugal. Since the beginning of this week, as I said before, both Portugal and Spain are finalizing the design of an exceptional and temporary measure to fix a reference price for gas used for the production of electricity in our country. This measure will be notified to the European Commission before the end of the week. We hope that the approval by Brussels will take place in a very short period of time and, as soon as we have the agreement of the Community institutions, the measure will be approved instantly in the State Official Gazette, with immediate effects on the light bills of our compatriots. The second measure is the updating of the specific remuneration regime for the production of electricity from renewable energy sources, cogeneration and waste, the one known as Recorre. With the approval of this measure, which may sound very technical, from the point of view of the reduction of charges on the electricity system will be a reduction of €1800 million this year, so that these charges will be reduced by 55 % in 2022. Thirdly, the mechanism for reducing excess profits in the electricity market caused by the high price of natural gas quotations on international markets has been extended until 30 June, with certain modifications to strengthen efficiency and adapt it to the European guidelines. Therefore, we will be even more robust, more effective in the face of the so-called fallen benefits of the sky of large electrical corporations in our country. [[Applauds]] These measures are accompanied by other regulatory measures which are important from a structural point of view, which involve the acceleration, deployment of renewable energy in our country, energy savings, the guarantee of energy supply, but which in any case in political terms convey a strong message from the Government of Spain, and it is our commitment to the green agenda, not to turn back on the green agenda and therefore to bet on energy autonomy based on renewable energy, efficiency and energy saving. Ladies and gentlemen, the general review of these measures shows that this is an ambitious crisis response plan, which effectively responds in very different areas to the effects of the war, but also gives a global response to the effects of the war in our country. After the two long years we have been through facing an unexpected and unpredictable pandemic, it is not necessary to say that no emergency plan can be considered definitive; this is obvious. Remember that in my last appearance I called this plan open and dynamic; open to your lordships. I would like to thank the contributions that the various parliamentary groups have made over these two weeks, as well as the autonomous communities, the municipalities, the social partners, the sectors particularly affected by the economic consequences of this war, but, of course, our wish is that the war will last as little time as possible and that as soon as possible we will recover the normality of economic recovery and the creation of such intense employment as we have undertaken. But if this is not the case, ladies and gentlemen, if the war is prolonged and there are other consequences, the Spanish Government will not hesitate to act again. I repeat, our unitary will in the approval and development of this necessary and ambitious plan is blunt. After a global pandemic, a number of natural disasters, including the eruption of a volcano, and now a war in Europe, we must ask ourselves again: what else has to happen if we are to respond together? Europe has understood it, Spain must understand it and the Government is going to turn, ladies and gentlemen, to achieve this maximum unity in the General Courts. [[Applauds]] Ladies and gentlemen, I shall now turn to the last point of my speech, which is the new stage which is opening up in relations between Spain and Morocco and which has been the subject of debate in the media and on the part of your ladies and gentlemen in various parliamentary speeches. I will start with an obvious thing, ladies and gentlemen, and it is that Morocco, which is a geographically neighbouring country, is above all an indispensable strategic partner, and has been accumulating throughout the years of history human ties, agreements, links, which have been forging shared interests over time. There have been periods of all kinds, there have been periods of greater stability, times of greater gloomyness in which the two countries, Spain and Morocco, and their citizens have certainly lost out. In the diplomatic crisis of 2002, with the occupation of the islet of Perejil, under a government of another sign, of the People's Party, the position of the socialist opposition was clear. That is precisely what former President Zapatero conveyed when he addressed this House from this rostrum. First, he expressed support for the Government, which was then chaired by José María Aznar. Secondly, he defended dialogue and diplomacy as essential formulas for finding a solution to that dispute, because, ladies and gentlemen, relations with Morocco are a matter of State, which requires a policy of State. As I have already said, and as the Foreign Minister also shared in his appearance in Parliament last week, the Spanish Government has the firm will to open a new stage in relations between Spain and Morocco, a new stage which will have a clear, ambitious road map, which, in my opinion, will enable us to close a crisis that has been dragging on in recent times and which, I insist, has been detrimental to the two countries. This has been my objective, ladies and gentlemen, the objective of my Government. The Government has two clear objectives, ladies and gentlemen, in its foreign policy: firstly, to defend the interests of Spain - I insist, to defend the interests of Spain - and, secondly, to do so by always adjusting the coordinates of the European Union and the multilateral order expressed in the resolutions of the United Nations Security Council. Much has been said, ladies and gentlemen, in recent days about a shift in the position of the Government in Morocco. Rather, I would say that we must speak of one more step on a path that began 14 years ago, when the Spanish Government welcomed the Moroccan proposal for autonomy as a valuable contribution to overcoming a conflict - let us not be fooled, ladies and gentlemen - which has been enquished for more than four decades. Nor, ladies and gentlemen, is there any disinterest in, or neglect of, the suffering of the Saharan people, what is there is a firm intention to contribute to the overcoming of a conflict that was overtaken half a century ago and with no prospect of resolution, at least until now, and all this always within the framework of the United Nations and with the agreement of the parties directly involved. What is more, it is the will to establish with Morocco the relations which correspond to two neighbouring countries with such strategic importance in the field of migration control, economic and trade relations, and also the fight against terrorism. In this regard, ladies and gentlemen, I expressed in my letter to King Mohamed VI the intention to build - and I quote textually - 'a relationship based on transparency and permanent communication, mutual respect and respect for the agreements signed by both parties, and the abstention of any unilateral action to live up to the importance of everything we share and to avoid future crises between our countries'. [[Applauds]] The Minister for Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation is going to travel to Rabat this Friday to launch the road map that consolidates this new relationship. Firstly, what we are going to do is to ensure a secure territory, with a border capable of ensuring that people and goods are controlled. An interministerial group has been formed, ladies and gentlemen, to put forward scenarios, options for reopening borders, including a protocol defining the criterion of entry into Spanish territory and ensuring safe and smooth traffic. Secondly, we will strengthen the mobilization and commitment of the State to Ceuta and Melilla. As you know, both special plans are being drawn up within the mandate set by the National Security Strategy, with the participation of all the institutions, of the entire association structure of these two autonomous cities, and this plan will logically be accompanied by national economic resources and from the European Union's recovery funds. As you know, I was in Ceuta and Melilla last May 2021, after the massive arrival of irregular immigrants in very few hours to Ceuta. On 23 March I had the opportunity to return and I did so with a solution to the problems we face. I therefore reiterate the path we have taken, which is that of real policy, the sense of state, the stability, prosperity and security of our country and also of two autonomous cities as important as Ceuta and Melilla. Ladies and gentlemen, I do not want to fail to address directly the issue that has moved more sensitivities, the question of Western Sahara. Again, in my letter to King Mohamed VI I acknowledged - and I quote again textually - 'the importance of the question of Western Sahara to Morocco and the serious and credible efforts that Morocco, within the framework of the United Nations, is making to find a mutually acceptable solution'. Then I added - and I quote textually -: 'In this sense, Spain considers the Moroccan proposal of autonomy presented in 2007 as the most serious, credible and realistic basis for the resolution of that dispute'; dispute, ladies and gentlemen, once again insists, that it is already forty-six years and that we must descale. I would like to explain these words very carefully, because I want all the Spanish and Spanish, and also your ladies and gentlemen, to understand with total clarity the position we have taken on this issue and why. First, ladies and gentlemen, in my letter he reaffirmed that the United Nations is the framework for resolving the conflict in Western Sahara. Spain has always supported and will always support the United Nations framework, because it is the only possible framework, because there is no other framework, and in turn Spain supports that the solution to the conflict is a mutually accepted solution by the parties, because that is also the only possible solution. Only the parties can decide whether the proposed solution is accepted by them, no one else. It is within this framework that I have just described, ladies and gentlemen, in which we logically acknowledge Morocco's efforts to reach a solution. Secondly, after establishing the framework for the solution - that is, the United Nations - in the letter stated, as I have just read, that we believe that the Moroccan proposal of 2007 - we are in the year 2022 - is the most serious, credible and realistic basis for the resolution of this dispute, a solution that must be achieved within the framework of the United Nations between the parties. What the Spanish Government believes is that, after many years of conflict and many years of stagnation in the negotiation process, this is the basis on which there is more chance of building, with the mediation - I insist again - of the United Nations and within the framework of its resolutions - I say this once again - a solution that can be accepted by the parties. The solution must be facilitated by the United Nations following a negotiation process, it must be adapted by the parties at the end of that process, and the rest of the international community, including Spain, must help the United Nations to move this negotiation process forward. Thirdly, I would like to stress that this is a position aligned with that of our European partners and with many other countries. France has supported Morocco's proposal for years; Germany has recently endorsed it through a letter sent by the President of the German Republic to the king of Morocco last January, as I did a few weeks ago. The European Commission, the High Representative and Vice-President of the European Union have expressed support for the position expressed by the Government of Spain. Also the United States - we just saw it yesterday - has recognized that Morocco's plan is serious, credible, realistic, and this is the position expressed by the Biden Administration, as I have just recalled. But, moreover, all the resolutions of the United Nations Security Council since 2007 take note of the Moroccan and Frente Polisario proposals and welcome - and I quote textually - the resolution of this United Nations Security Council - I insist, textually - 'with satisfaction Morocco's serious and credible efforts to move forward in the process towards a solution'. I would also like to recall that this is a clear position, but it is not new with regard to the positions expressed by previous governments in Spain. Spain had already recognized the autonomy plan in 2007, when it was presented precisely by the Moroccan authorities to the then President Zapatero. Joint statements adopted at the high-level meetings between the Government of Spain and the Government of Morocco, held in Morocco, chaired by Mariano Rajoy in 2012 and 2015, recognized Morocco's efforts and disappeared any reference to the right to self-determination. In particular, in 2012, ladies and gentlemen, the following was said in text: 'Spain has also welcomed Morocco's serious and credible efforts'. And in 2015 that phrase was repeated, which is also what I used in my letter to King Mohamed VI. Ladies and gentlemen, I am not underestimating the decision I have taken, I have taken it with the full will to take a step forward. However, it is appropriate to point out that when the Presidents of the Government of different political signs have met with this question of Western Sahara they have always understood its enormous complexity, and I ask you to appreciate that complexity as well. [[Applauds]] We have reaffirmed, we have deepened our position precisely now that a new window of opportunity has just been opened to resume negotiations. It's said, why now? Because now a window of opportunity has been opened, ladies and gentlemen; because the parties can achieve, after forty-six years, a solution to the conflict or, at least, open a negotiation process; because a new personal envoy of the Secretary-General of the United Nations for Western Sahara, Staffan de Mistura, has recently been appointed - last October - who has renewed the hope that these negotiations can be resumed. After two years without the appointment of a new special envoy by the Secretary-General of the United Nations - up to thirteen candidates, ladies and gentlemen, had previously been rejected - there is again one that has been well received by the parties, which enjoys the full support of the international community and, without a doubt, also of our country. Spain wants to take an active, non-passive role and actively support the work of the new special envoy Staffan de Mistura. I would like to conclude this chapter, ladies and gentlemen, by reaffirming and reiterating Spain's support for the Saharan people. We are the main donor of humanitarian aid to the Saharan refugee camps, and be assured that we will continue to provide them with such humanitarian support. [[Applauds]] The conflict in Western Sahara has already condemned too many generations of Saharans to poverty, to lack of opportunity. I hope that, under the efforts of the new special envoy of the Secretary-General of the United Nations, progress can be made in the negotiations, and Spain will certainly lend, as it is already providing, support in order to do so. Ladies and gentlemen, I conclude by thanking you for your attention. It is a fact that in just twenty-six months of the legislature we have had to deal with a chained succession of emergencies of extraordinary depth. The worst global pandemic of the last century, various migration crises, natural disasters with the La Palma volcano paradigm, the evacuation of Afghanistan and now the Russian war in Ukraine; extraordinary events that have forced us, without rest, to take extraordinary measures, so I would like to thank each and every member of the Council of Ministers for the work they have carried out over these long months of legislature. [[Applauds]] It is also a fact that we have responded to these challenges, as I said before, with measures based on justice, unity and determination. In justice, especially based on a welfare state that has demonstrated, with swift and effective measures, that the old recipes, based on cuts and social exclusion, are not the way. The axes of transformation have been established, by which the world is going to move forward and by which Spain cannot be detached: digitisation, energy transformation and, without a doubt, the qualification of our human capital, of our young people in order to guarantee their employability and also their intellectual autonomy in a charged world - as we are seeing in recent weeks especially - of misinformation. And we have reached historic agreements, such as the European Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, which ultimately means debt-sharing at European level, which will have a profound modernising effect on our country. But now, without a solution of continuity, we are suddenly faced with a nightmare that we thought had been overcome since World War II on our continent, as is the return of war to the heart of Europe. Ladies and gentlemen, nothing of the debate that we are going to hold can and must therefore ignore this absolutely indisputable fact, and it is that we are facing an emergency that is going to define Spain and Europe in the coming decades. And, if our heart is at the side of the Ukrainian people, who suffer directly from the atrocity of this aggression perpetrated by a dictator like Putin, we cannot and must not forget another equally indisputable fact, and it is that the security, the well-being of our country is at risk if we do not act with roundness and determination. And I am not referring only to the various consequences of the invasion - from the reception of the refugees to the energy crisis we suffer - Putin's ordago to the international political, economic and social order poses a threat to the global destabilization that can affect the very course of the war but that can also lead us to imagine the worst of the scenarios as a long post-war period. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, I call once again on responsibility, as we have already done in the worst moments of the pandemic, I call again on intelligence, on capacity, on the common sense of all the political forces in this House, to understand well the gravity of the moment we live in. Ladies and gentlemen, we are faced with an unprecedented scenario, open to many possibilities, both positive and extraordinarily negative. We have to make decisions in the midst of the profound irrationality of a war, with all the uncertainty that this entails, and therefore we cannot get lost in minor debates and barren quarrels that take us away from that fundamental view. For once, therefore, on behalf of the Government, if I ask for something from your ladies and gentlemen, it is union, it is support. [[Applauds]] And I want to say to you, ladies and gentlemen, that I do not think that asking for support and unity in the midst of a pandemic and a war in Europe is too much asking. In exceptional times of unity and extraordinary solutions, we have tried this during these 20 months of the legislature, not always with the consensus that would have been desirable, but I do not lose hope. Let us do so this time; I ask you, ladies and gentlemen, for the good of Spain and for the good of Europe. Thank you. [[Applauds]] He also wished to welcome the diplomatic progress being made towards a peace agreement between Ukraine and Russia, particularly with the appointment of a mediator by the United Nations. I believe that everyone wants peace, especially the Ukrainian people. I believe that we must celebrate all and all that progress that I hope will soon bring about a peace agreement that will put an end to the war. Ladies and gentlemen, Mr President, these previous questions have entered into the subject of the debate we are debating here today. Today it is up to me to take on a complex intervention. By definition, coalitions are formed by political forces that coincide in some things, if it would not be impossible to coalition, and they disagree in so many others, if we were not the same political party, would be a one-party government and would not be a coalition. I know this is a grudge, but I feel obliged to say this grudge because we live in a day-to-day time when the scarecrows who hysterically cry out that we have betrayed our principles when we come to an agreement with the Socialist Party or start screaming that the government is broken every time we disagree. Well, not one thing or the other. It is something called a coalition and it is a fairly old invention in parliamentary democracies. I say that my speech is complex because today's debate is a good example of this happening in the coalitions. Today we are dealing with a matter on which we disagree, Mr President, and another issue on which we have many points in common. I will start with the first, with the one in which we have a disagreement with the will to end the good part of my intervention. I'm going to start with Western Sahara. Firstly, I wanted to establish clearly the terms of the debate. Much is being said and there are different views on whether or not there has been a change of position on this issue and indeed the diplomatic issues are very complex, but the existing positions on the settlement of the conflict in Western Sahara are very simple to understand, and I think we will have to start there. Basically, there are two positions. One, which defends the self-determination of the Saharan people, that is, if they so decide, they can form an independent State, and another is that which prefers Western Sahara to be one more province of the Moroccan regime, with some level of autonomy still to be defined. These are basically the two positions that exist, and I have not heard any other positions that are outside these two. The first, that of the right of self-determination, is the position of the UN, through resolutions 2625 and 1514, is the position of the United Podemos and it is also the one on the electoral agenda of the Socialist Party, where it is literally said to be committed to a solution of the conflict that is just, definitive, mutually acceptable and respectful of the principle of self-determination of the Saharan people. The second position, the position that the Sahara becomes one more province of the Moroccan regime with some level of autonomy yet to be defined, is Trump's position - let us not forget that Trump defined it, although now Mr. Biden maintains it - is obviously Morocco's position, as everyone knows, and now, unfortunately, from our point of view, is the position of the Government of Spain. If I am wrong, it is easy to deny myself, it would be enough for the Government to say that it is committed to the self-determination of the Saharan people. I do not think that will happen because, for our parliamentary group, unfortunately, there has been a change of position. Furthermore, we think that the new position is difficult to justify, we think so and I say it to you, Mr President, with all the loyalty; first of all, because of the enormous solidarity that exists in Spain with the Saharan people, which I also believe is cross-cutting to all political ideologies, but also for more reasons. Right now, an autocrat, an autocratic, authoritarian regime, has invaded a smaller country. Russia has invaded Ukraine and is violating human rights in Ukrainian territory, and every West agrees that claims of Russian sovereignty over Crimea or Donbas are not legitimate because they violate international law. In the case of the Sahara, we also have an autocratic regime, which is Morocco, which has invaded a smaller country, which is Western Sahara, which is violating human rights in that territory and does not claim sovereignty over a part of the territory but over all of it. We believe that, in the same way that international law is to be defended in Ukraine, it must also be defended in Western Sahara. [[Applauds]] Besides, I think we have a double responsibility. You have quoted the diplomatic position of several European countries, but in our case it is very special. We are the decolonizing power and our position is much more important than the position of Germany or the position of France. I think we need to address more arguments, but I will only put two more on the table. The first you have mentioned it and it has been mentioned a lot these days; it is true that it is a conflict that has been overtaken and that it would be good for you to move forward, this is correct and we share it, and it is certain that the Saharan people also share it, but it is doubtful that a solution of autonomy will solve it; in fact, there is a single precedent of decolonization based on autonomy that went very badly, that is Eritrea. A solution based on autonomy was proposed, and what happened next was that the Ethiopian State suppressed Eritrea's autonomy, there was a bloody war and it ended up declaring Eritrea's independence, that is, it is not clear even in terms of realpolitik, as has been raised here today, that this will lead to a solution to this conflict. Finally, I believe that when a decision by a government raises so little understanding on the part of our country's citizens, when it is so little shared, when it is not understood, I believe that the government has a duty to listen and reflect, and I believe that in this case it should do so, and I also say this to you, Mr President, with all loyalty. Here we have heard, as always, the hyperventilated right and extreme right groups and that, in the face of any discrepancy that we coalition partners have, they intend to break the government, but we intend to take care of the coalition, and I believe that one way to take care of it is that, when we have a discrepancy, let us say it and express it with total loyalty and honesty, and I believe that is what I humbly have just done. Turning to the second issue we are dealing with today, we also believe that taking care of the government coalition is courageous action. They know perfectly well that with us in the government, measures are going to be taken to protect and improve the lives of people, the lives of most people, and that, which is repeated and repeated and which has already happened many times in this legislature, panics them because that completely disassembles the story, that story that they are good managers, the story that when a political formation like United We can be in the government, chaos will arise. However, since we ruled we have limited, we have in fact prohibited the sale to losses in the field - that is a market intervention with all those of the law -, we have touched the fallen profits of the electric sky, which are a negative effect of the design of the marginalist market - that is a market intervention with all those of the law - we have limited the price of the masks - that is a market intervention - and now with this real law decree we have carried out a massive intervention of the rental market. I think that today, with the figure of inflation, which is scary, we see perfectly what this means: it would have been absolutely tragic that the rents of the families in Spain would have increased by more than 9% with which it is falling, and precisely with this courageous measure that we have carried out we have managed to avoid that, we have managed that the 10% increase in rents, which can be 60 or 80 euros a month for an average family, does not take place. ParlaMint-ES_2022-03-30-CD220330.u232	Mr President, Minister, ladies and gentlemen, you raised this question with seriousness and respect to speak at the Congress of the Deputies of the State Anonymous Postal and Telegraph Society, the postal public operator, the largest Spanish public company, one of the main companies in the country by size, capillary and human capital, no less than 52,456 employees throughout the Group of Postal Affairs. It is a provider of physical, digital and parcel communications, and is the public operator designated to provide the Universal Postal Service in Spain, depending on this reason on the Ministry of Transport, but also, as a member of the public business sector, integrated into the SEPI Group, under the control of the Ministry of Finance and Public Service. Minister, we are extraordinarily concerned about the management of Post Offices, in particular of President Juan Manuel Serrano Quintana, who with no experience of business management was appointed in July 2018 by Pedro Sánchez, according to criteria of friendship and political coincidence, since he was his chief of staff in the Federal Executive Committee of the Socialist Party. Ladies and gentlemen, Serrano Quintana is leading the Group of the European People's Party to a critical situation of difficult reversibility, unless the Government reacts immediately. The demoralization and discouragement of people working in Post Offices are capitalized. Minister, are you aware of the critical financial and economic situation of Post Offices, near the collapse? In the year 2020 the State Postal Company had a catastrophic result, with a negative operating result of 353 million euros and losses of 256.68 million euros. Indeed, the year 2020 was a year in which the effects of the COVID-19 pandemic were felt and there was a setback, a decline in what is the traditional postal segment, however, in the area of messaging and parcelry the year 2020 was a year of growth in general. According to the annual report of the postal sector of the National Securities Market Commission of 2020, operators in this parcel and courier segment grew by 17,6 %. Well, Minister, the turnover of the public operator in this segment fell by 12.9% compared to the previous year. That is to say, in the year of the explosion of electronic commerce by the confinements, the public operator loses turnover in relation to the previous year, as its competitors, as I said, grow. Ladies and gentlemen, the losses of this postal company in the year 2021, in the year of recovery, in the year when things began to improve, will probably reach EUR 100 million. But the figure is even higher if you increase the sale, which was made almost the last 3 Words withdrawn by the Presidency in accordance with Rule 104.3 of the Rules of Procedure of the House. See page 105. day of the year to make up the accounts, of the main building of Correos de Valencia for 26 million euros; a regrettable way to decapitalize the company. The company's cashflow is negative for the first time in society's history. Credits amounting to EUR 433 million had to be requested to cover short-term payments; a credit line of EUR 600 million had to be requested, because the group's debts had been multiplied by ninety times by the fall in its own funds. Ladies and gentlemen, if these decisions are not corrected in the short term, the value of the company's liability will exceed the asset and Post will go into technical bankruptcy, and the government that led to the appointment of Serrano will be the last responsible. What control is the State Society of Industrial Participation doing of what I have just said? Minister, are you aware of the irrational policy of human resources and decapitalisation that is taking place in the company? Only in the last four months have a total of 20 managers been dismissed with conciliation in the mediation service, many of them with truly scandalous compensations. And worst of all, these people are immediately registered by competition companies, which clearly take advantage of the know-how and knowledge of these people. The President of the Post Office, Mr Serrano, is appointing for senior management positions persons who do not have the necessary qualifications to be in charge of the responsibilities entrusted to them, and we even believe that there are appointments that have been made without the authorization of SEPI. Much of the leadership of the Correos Express directive has gone to competition companies, have been registered, as I just told you a moment ago, and some, like the Portuguese company that has hosted these managers, have grown in 2020 by 70% in activity and 41% in billing. President Serrano, friend of Pedro Sánchez, has not been able to stabilize a management team to manage a company of the size and relevance of Post Offices. How is it justified that since the arrival of this responsibility, in the Presidency of Posts, there have been three Directors-General of Technology, three Directors-General of Commerce, three Directors of Human Resources and that those who carry out this responsibility do so provisionally since 2011? How is it possible for these people to be remunerated with salaries in excess of EUR 100 000? Madam Minister, are you aware of the policy of giving up correspondence and the policy of erratic diversification of Posts? Since his appointment as President of the Post Office, Mr Serrano has questioned everything he has done until his arrival, and carried out a simplistic analysis, in which it was concluded that the letters, the traditional correspondence, was to disappear, when in 2019 it represented 65% of the company's revenue. Communications with physical support since the presidency Mr Serrano has fallen to bits, above 40%. In no country in Europe has the physical replacement of the letter by electronic means occurred so quickly. Nor can the unbridled pricing policy that has been followed in Posts be viewed as positive. It has undoubtedly been the most active agent for the electronic replacement of the letter: price increases that have gone far above inflation and have clearly damaged the profitability of the company, because it has become much weaker before competitors. There are products, such as one with high profitability, advertising, which has recorded increases of 60%. So it's not possible to compete. And, with regard to diversification, of course diversification is necessary, but it has to be done with planning, it has to be done with control, it has to be done with caution, not in the way it is being proposed. And, as for the package, that segment of which I spoke to you, Minister, in which there has been growth, as with the sector as a whole, as a result of the company's cost structure, as a result of not being able to plan an adequate growth strategy and by that price policy, has been lost large parcel customers. Minister, are you aware of the failures in the policy of internationalization of the State Postal Society? Are you aware that the internationalization, for example, that has been raised with China, with the participation in a consolidating company, has not yet produced any kind of performance or benefit to the company Correos, to the company that has acquired that participation, and that such important customers as AliExpress, who was a huge parcel supplier, have been lost? Only the loss of this customer, not being able to offer it the right services and the right prices, has entailed losses of more than 5.5 million units of packages in 2011. The same applies to the investment in the purchase of a Portuguese subsidiary of Correos Express Portugal, which reports only 0.1 % of its total turnover to the Groupe Correos. I remind you, gentlemen of the PSOE, that our income is already 13% lower than that of our European neighbours. Energy costs are increasing and, at the same time, we diverge in terms of per capita income with our European neighbours. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, that the difference in the Spanish inflation rate vis-à-vis our partners is two more points and that prices rise by 40% more in Spain than in Europe is a real drama, not only because it impoverishes families in the present, but because it also condemns us in the future by making us a less competitive economy.
